/bin/zsh -lc "sed -n '1,220p' outputs/reconstructions/step-to-cadquery/gravity-broom-holder/main.py" in /Users/macbookpro/Documents/ai-chain/_hardware/cad-skill-test-2
 succeeded in 0ms:
"""Recovered "Gravity Broom Holder" (LoboCNC, Printables #152557) — cadcode project.

2 independent solids, built directly in the SOURCE STEP's own coordinate frame
(no re-centering, no extra placement transform needed — see params.py):

  solid 0  gear/roller   scaffold = plain hollow cylinder (hub + bore);
                         residual = the 18 involute-ish teeth (genuinely
                         freeform — see spec.md / pitfalls.md)
  solid 1  rack/body     scaffold = top mounting slab + 2 full-height leg
                         walls + guide-arch fillet + 2 keyhole-slot circles +
                         7x2 nub-hole grid + outer edge fillets;
                         residual = the tilted 15 deg ratchet wall + its 14
                         cylindrical scallops, the true (non-circular) keyhole
                         outline, the front lip, and the top nub (all
                         deliberately left to the frozen import — see
                         spec.md "Residual-frozen regions")

gen_step() builds each solid's scaffold, fast_unions its residual sidecar (if
present), and returns both as a compound — the two solids are disjoint
(never touch), so no boolean is needed BETWEEN them.
"""

import math
import os

import cadquery as cq

from params import Params
from validation import validate_params
from booleans import fast_union


def here(rel: str) -> str:
    return os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), rel)


def as_solid(wp: cq.Workplane) -> cq.Workplane:
    """Collapse a Workplane wrapping a Compound-of-one-Solid (what fast_union /
    fast_cut return) down to a bare Solid object. CadQuery's `.cut()`/`.fillet()`
    etc. call `findSolid()`, which only recognises a `Solid` instance on the
    stack, not a `Compound` — so every `fast_union`/`fast_cut` result must be
    collapsed before it's used as the receiver of another Workplane-level op."""
    solids = wp.val().Solids()
    if len(solids) == 1:
        return cq.Workplane(obj=solids[0])
    return wp


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Solid 0 — gear / roller scaffold (hollow cylinder only; teeth are the wrap)
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def build_gear_scaffold(p: Params):
    body = (
        cq.Workplane("XY")
        .center(p.gear_cx, p.gear_cy)
        .circle(p.gear_hub_od / 2.0)
        .circle(p.gear_bore_d / 2.0)
        .extrude(p.gear_h)
        .translate((0, 0, p.gear_z0))
    )
    return body


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Solid 1 — rack / body scaffold
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def build_rack_scaffold(p: Params):
    # 1) Top mounting slab + two full-height legs — each box gets its own
    #    TRUE-OUTER edges filleted INDIVIDUALLY, before any union. Filleting
    #    the whole post-union body in one `.edges().fillet()` call is what
    #    the original attempt tried, and it silently produced an INVALID
    #    solid (BRepCheck_Analyzer False, no exception raised — a bare
    #    try/except does not catch this class of failure; see pitfalls.md)
    #    once 3 boxes' seams and corners were all in the same edge list. A
    #    plain box's own outer edges fillet cleanly every time, so each
    #    box is filleted alone, THEN unioned — the union simply reproduces
    #    the already-rounded corners, since the fillet radius is small
    #    relative to each box.
    # Derived coordinates from origin+width params (never two independent
    # absolute coordinates that could invert under a perturb nudge — see
    # params.py docstring and pitfalls.md).
    body_x1 = p.body_x0 + p.body_w
    leg_l_x1 = p.body_x0 + p.leg_l_w
    leg_r_x0 = body_x1 - p.leg_r_w

    r = p.fillet_outer
    slab = (
        cq.Workplane("XY")
        .box(
            body_x1 - p.body_x0,
            p.body_y1 - p.body_y0,
            p.body_z1 - p.slab_z0,
            centered=(False, False, False),
        )
        .translate((p.body_x0, p.body_y0, p.slab_z0))
    )
    slab = slab.edges("|Z").fillet(r)          # slab's 4 vertical corners
    slab = slab.edges(">Z").fillet(r)          # slab's top perimeter

    leg_l = (
        cq.Workplane("XY")
        .box(
            leg_l_x1 - p.body_x0,
            p.body_y1 - p.body_y0,
            p.body_z1 - p.body_z0,
            centered=(False, False, False),
        )
        .translate((p.body_x0, p.body_y0, p.body_z0))
    )
    # Only the true-outer vertical edges (x=body_x0) are filleted with
    # OCCT's fillet operator here. The OTHER 2 vertical edges (at
    # x=leg_l_x1, where the inner/channel-facing face meets the front/back
    # faces) are ALSO real outer edges of the part and DO overshoot if left
    # perfectly sharp — but OCCT's fillet raises `BRep_API: command not
    # done` there once the guide-arch's big (8.89mm) fillet is anywhere
    # nearby (tried both fillet orderings; see pitfalls.md). Fixed instead
    # with small manual corner-notch cutters after the guide-arch fillet,
    # below — plain boolean box cuts are far more robust than a second
    # fillet operator fighting the first one for the same edge region.
    leg_l = leg_l.edges("|Z").filter(lambda e: abs(e.Center().x - p.body_x0) < 0.1).fillet(r)
    leg_l_bottom = leg_l.edges("<Z").filter(lambda e: (
        abs(e.Center().x - p.body_x0) < 1.0
        or abs(e.Center().y - p.body_y0) < 0.1
        or abs(e.Center().y - p.body_y1) < 0.1
    ))
    if leg_l_bottom.vals():
        leg_l = leg_l.newObject(leg_l_bottom.vals()).fillet(r)

    leg_r = (
        cq.Workplane("XY")
        .box(
            body_x1 - leg_r_x0,
            p.body_y1 - p.body_y0,
            p.body_z1 - p.body_z0,
            centered=(False, False, False),
        )
        .translate((leg_r_x0, p.body_y0, p.body_z0))
    )
    # leg_r is a thin (~2.6mm) captured slice (see params.py note) — too
    # narrow for a 1.35mm fillet on BOTH vertical edges at once (they'd
    # collide and OCCT raises `BRep_API: command not done`). Only the true
    # outer corner (x=body_x1) gets filleted; the inner (channel-facing)
    # corner is already deep in undershoot territory, so it's left sharp
    # (safe — sharp is a subset of the real rounded corner's material).
    leg_r = leg_r.edges("|Z").filter(lambda e: abs(e.Center().x - body_x1) < 0.1).fillet(r)
    leg_r_bottom = leg_r.edges("<Z").filter(lambda e: (
        abs(e.Center().x - body_x1) < 1.0
        or abs(e.Center().y - p.body_y0) < 0.1
        or abs(e.Center().y - p.body_y1) < 0.1
    ))
    if leg_r_bottom.vals():
        try:
            leg_r = leg_r.newObject(leg_r_bottom.vals()).fillet(r)
        except Exception:
            pass

    base = as_solid(fast_union(slab, leg_l))
    base = as_solid(fast_union(base, leg_r))

    # 3) Guide-arch relief: a single quarter-round fillet on the inner-top
    #    edge of the LEFT (guide) leg, running the full Y depth — the real
    #    feature per introspect.py (radius ~8.89mm, axis Y, sweep 90deg).
    #    Select the edge by its location (parallel to Y, at X=leg_l_x1,
    #    Z=slab_z0), not by a bare `edges("|Y")` (that would also match
    #    every other Y-parallel edge on the block).
    guide_edges = (
        base.edges("|Y")
        .filter(lambda e: (
            abs(e.Center().x - leg_l_x1) < 0.5
            and abs(e.Center().z - p.slab_z0) < 0.5
        ))
    )
    try:
        if guide_edges.vals():
            base = as_solid(base.newObject(guide_edges.vals()).fillet(p.guide_arch_r))
    except Exception:
        pass

    # 3b) Small corner-notch cutters at the 4 inner-vertical corners a
    #     second OCCT fillet pass can't safely reach (leg_l_x1 x {front,
    #     back}, leg_r_x0 x {front, back}) — a plain conservative box cut,
    #     sized bigger than the true ~1.35mm fillet sagitta, guarantees no
    #     overshoot there without fighting OCCT's fillet solver twice on
    #     the same edge region (see the note above build_rack_scaffold).
    notch = 2.0
    for cx in (leg_l_x1, leg_r_x0):
        for cy, y_start in ((p.body_y0, p.body_y0), (p.body_y1, p.body_y1 - notch)):
            cutter = (
                cq.Workplane("XY")
                .box(notch, notch, (p.body_z1 - p.body_z0) + 2.0, centered=(True, False, False))
                .translate((cx, y_start, p.body_z0 - 1.0))
            )
            base = as_solid(base.cut(cutter))

    # 4) Two keyhole mount slots — a rectangular cutter sized to the keyhole's
    #    own measured bounding box (a SUPERSET of the true hex/bulb outline —
    #    see params.py note on why cutouts must over-cut, not under-cut, to
    #    avoid overshoot). Built as an explicit cutter solid + plain .cut()
    #    (not cutThruAll — the fast_union-returned Workplane doesn't carry
    #    the parent-solid context findSolid() needs).
    cx0 = p.mount_cx_mid - p.mount_spacing / 2.0
    cx1 = p.mount_cx_mid + p.mount_spacing / 2.0
    keyhole_h = (p.body_z1 - p.slab_z0) + 2.0
    keyholes = (
        cq.Workplane("XY")
        .pushPoints([(cx0, p.mount_cy), (cx1, p.mount_cy)])
        .rect(p.mount_slot_x, p.mount_slot_y)
        .extrude(keyhole_h)
        .translate((0, 0, p.slab_z0 - 1.0))
    )
    base = as_solid(base.cut(keyholes))

    # 5) 7x2 drywall-anchor-nub hole grid through the LEFT leg (X-normal
    #    face), matching introspect's `lattices` entry almost exactly
    #    (14 cells, pitch 6.35/3.175mm, 1.648mm square). One box cutter per
    #    hole, unioned then cut in one shot (plain booleans — simple
    #    axis-aligned boxes, no coincident-freeform degeneracy risk).
    nub_depth = (leg_l_x1 - p.body_x0) + 2.0
    nub_cutters = []
    for col in range(int(round(p.nub_cols))):

No evaluator helper/scoring-policy change was made. The batch did not expose the loop-report ball-socket seating/articulation candidate; the recurring gaps were placed-instance extraction from STEP/showcase STLs, external object behavior, and joint/contact simulation. Before/after evaluator behavior is therefore unchanged.

Helper usage in this batch: `assembly_component_count`, `clear_path_proxy`, `cylindrical_fit`.

Remaining missing-helper themes: gear/broom-handle gravity locking, filament hinge rotation, tab/lid seating, battery rolling/queueing, external dispenser cartridge seating, press-fit/friction retention, and placed-instance separation/contact checks.

Unused checks such as `part_collision`, `linear_motion_collision`, `linear_motion_clearance`, `rotation_motion_collision`, `relative_pose`, `vent_grid_open_area_proxy`, and `contact_graph` were absent from this batch, not proven useless. They need placed instance meshes, motion axes, or grille/vent geometry that these four evaluations did not provide.

Temp cleanup is complete: `.eval_tmp` directories and temporary manifests/reports were removed. Verification: `PYTHONPATH=skills/evaluate-cad-reconstruction python3 -m unittest skills/evaluate-cad-reconstruction/tests/test_physical_correctness_score.py` passed 26 tests.
